# SS Lessons: Clinical Fact-Check Disagreements and Flags (Lane C)

Gate 4 blind fact-check discrepancies logged for owner clinical review.

| Lesson Key | Step | Fact Statement | Source in Blueprint | Checker Assessment | Resolution / Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sgi-oesophageal-motility | image steps | Lane B added two bank figures (bird-beak barium swallow; corkscrew oesophagus). Step text unchanged. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | No flags. Chicago v4 cutoffs, POEM, DES, jackhammer, and Chagas were left as written. Image captions were not flagged. |
| sgi-hpylori-peptic-ulcer | image steps | Lane B added two bank figures (H. pylori outcomes; ulcer and NSAID barrier). Step text unchanged. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Fixed three errors. Eradication before extensive metaplasia cuts later cancer risk but does not erase it. The stool antigen test detects shed antigen, not urease. PGE2 and PGI2 are not prostaglandins 1 and 2. Maastricht doses were not flagged. |
| sgi-upper-gi-bleeding | steps 1, 4, 5, 7 | Coverage rewrite so all 8 mustKnow facts are in the lesson. Kept both bank images. Added restrictive transfusion (haemoglobin 7 to 9 g/dL), intravenous PPI 80 mg then 8 mg/hour for 72 hours to pH above 6.0, Dieulafoy caliber-persistent artery, over-the-scope clips for ulcers larger than 1.5 cm, and the HALT-IT tranexamic acid result. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Sleisenger 11e Ch. 20 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | One medium flag rejected. The checker read the restrictive haemoglobin target of 7 to 9 g/dL as a liberal trigger. That range is the Villanueva post-transfusion target. HALT-IT, Forrest, and the PPI infusion were not flagged. No text change. |
| sgi-sibo-tropical-sprue | steps 2, 4, 5 | Coverage rewrite. Kept both bank images. Named hydrogen breath tests and parts per million, called rifaximin a non-absorbed antibiotic at 550 mg 3 times daily for 14 days, stated deconjugation of bile salts as the cause of fat malabsorption, and added Whipple disease (Tropheryma whipplei, PAS-positive macrophages). | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Sleisenger 11e Ch. 108 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | No flags. The 10^3 CFU/mL aspirate cutoff, 20 ppm hydrogen rise, rifaximin 550 mg three times daily for 14 days, and Whipple PAS macrophages were left as written. |
| sgi-crohn-disease | steps 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 | Coverage rewrite. Kept both bank images. Added fissuring ulcers and granulomas in 40% to 60% of resections, the phrase Perianal Crohn disease, endoscopic balloon dilatation for a straight stricture of 5 cm or less, creeping fat (fat wrapping) over 50% of the circumference, the Rutgeerts score at 6 months (i2 or more than 5 aphthous ulcers), and extraintestinal manifestations including erythema nodosum. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Sleisenger 11e Ch. 114 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Checker did not flag Rutgeerts. I still corrected it: i2 is more than 5 aphthous ulcers in the neoterminal ileum, and i2 or higher is the step-up threshold. The old sentence treated every higher score as if it were only that definition. |
| sgi-colorectal-neoplasia | steps 1, 3, 7, 8 | Coverage rewrite. Kept both bank images. Added adenoma detection rate targets (25% overall, 30% men, 20% women, 1% rise cuts interval cancer by 3%), STK11 mutations, post-polypectomy surveillance at 3 years for 3 or more adenomas, size 10 mm, tubulovillous histology, or high-grade dysplasia, and first-line pembrolizumab or dostarlimab for metastatic deficient mismatch repair disease. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Sleisenger 11e Ch. 126, 127 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Checker did not flag dostarlimab. I still corrected it: pembrolizumab is first-line for metastatic deficient mismatch repair colorectal cancer. Dostarlimab is a PD-1 antibody for dMMR tumours that have already progressed, not the first-line colorectal standard. Both names stay so the blueprint terms remain covered. |
| sgi-microscopic-colitis-anorectal | steps 3, 4, 6, 7 | Coverage rewrite. Kept both bank images and the 90% posterior-midline fissure caption. Wrote budesonide remission as 80%, added Ogilvie syndrome with neostigmine 2 mg over 5 minutes when the caecum exceeds 12 cm, solitary rectal ulcer syndrome with fibromuscular obliteration, internal hemorrhoid grades 1 to 4 by prolapse severity, and radiation proctopathy treated with argon plasma coagulation. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Sleisenger 11e Ch. 112, 128 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | No flags. Budesonide, neostigmine 2 mg when the caecum exceeds 12 cm, Goodsall, and the 90% posterior fissure were left as written. |
| sgi-pancreatic-neoplasms | image steps | Lane B added two bank figures (IPMN with mucin at the ampulla; insulinoma and Whipple triad). Step text unchanged. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Fixed the POLO step. The trial was maintenance olaparib in germline BRCA1/2 metastatic pancreatic adenocarcinoma after platinum, not neuroendocrine tumours and not somatic-only mutations. The blueprint fact that ties POLO to pNETs is wrong. Fukuoka 5 mm and 10 mm were not flagged. |

Gemini flagged Lyon Barrett >=1 cm as if only >=3 cm were conclusive, and flagged "amitriptyline for EPS type". | ss-gastroenterology blueprint, Lyon Consensus 2.0 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Kept >=1 cm. | sgi-oesophageal-cancer | images 0 and 3 | Junctional adenocarcinoma drawing plus the Barrett sequence. Gemini asked to start Howel-Evans surveillance at 30 rather than 20. | ss-gastroenterology blueprint | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low) | Kept age 20. ## Rheumatology Lane A Lessons Fact-Check Verification (2026-11-04)
Writer: gemini-3.8-flash-low | Checker: claude-sonnet-4-6 (blind fact check against Kelley & Firestein 11e, Harrison 22e, ACR/EULAR criteria)
All 27 topics authored by Lane A checked:
- srh-immune-basics: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). TLR4/7/9, NLRP3 inflammasome, Th1/Th2/Th17, FOXP3/IPEX, BAFF, IL-23/IL-17 axis, HLA-B27/DRB1 shared epitope, molecular mimicry verified.
- srh-synovial-fluid: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Arthrocentesis landmarks, WBC classification categories, polarized microscopy, Gram stain / culture verified.
- srh-secondary-immunodeficiency: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Rituximab hypogammaglobulinemia, glucocorticoids, MMF, nephrotic IgG loss, secondary infections verified.
- srh-ra-pathogenesis: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). PAD citrullination, ACPA/RF, synovial pannus, 2010 ACR/EULAR criteria verified.
- srh-csdmards: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Methotrexate mechanism/monitoring, leflunomide washout, sulfasalazine, hydroxychloroquine retinal screening verified.
- srh-still-disease: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Yamaguchi criteria, hyperferritinemia, glycosylated ferritin <20%, IL-1/IL-6 therapies verified.
- srh-psa: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). CASPAR criteria, peripheral/axial phenotypes, dactylitis, IL-17/IL-23 inhibitors verified.
- srh-gout: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Hyperuricemia crystallization, negative birefringence, acute flare management, allopurinol/febuxostat targets verified.
- srh-metabolic-bone: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). DEXA T-scores, FRAX thresholds, bisphosphonates, denosumab rebound, romosozumab, osteomalacia Looser zones verified.
- srh-sle-pathogenesis: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Apoptotic clearance failure, TLR7/9 pDC interferon signature, BAFF/APRIL, C1q deficiency, NETosis, complement consumption verified.
- srh-sle-management: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). HCQ <=5 mg/kg, LLDAS target, steroid tapering <=5 mg, MMF in nephritis, belimumab, anifrolumab, rituximab verified.
- srh-sjogren: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). 2016 ACR/EULAR criteria, labial focus score >=1, extraglandular features, distal RTA, MALT lymphoma risk, pilocarpine/cevimeline verified.
- srh-raynaud: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Triphasic color sequence, primary vs secondary, nailfold capillaroscopy, nifedipine, PDE-5 inhibitors, iloprost/bosentan verified.
- srh-igg4: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). 2019 criteria >=20 pts, storiform fibrosis/obliterative phlebitis, IgG4/IgG >40%, retroperitoneal fibrosis, prednisone/rituximab verified.
- srh-vasculitis-classification: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). 2012 CHCC caliber definitions, 2022 ACR/EULAR sets, BVAS v3 vs VDI, Five-Factor Score domains verified.
- srh-pan: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Medium artery necrotizing arteritis, pulmonary/glomerular sparing, HBV association, microaneurysms beads-on-a-string, FFS-directed therapy verified.
- srh-mpa: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Pauci-immune crescentic GN, MPO-ANCA, absence of granulomatous ENT disease, PEXIVAS findings, rituximab/cyclophosphamide verified.
- srh-anti-gbm: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Alpha-3(IV)NC1 autoantibodies, linear IgG immunofluorescence, HLA-DRB1*15:01, plasma exchange + cyclophosphamide verified.
- srh-single-organ: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). Cogan keratitis + vestibuloauditory, aortitis, PACNS biopsy, cutaneous LCV, glucocorticoids/infliximab verified.
- srh-autoinflammatory: Checked (claude-sonnet-4-6). MEFV/pyrin FMF, NLRP3 CAPS, TNFRSF1A TRAPS, MVK HIDS, NOD2 Blau, PSTPIP1 PAPA, SAVI interferonopathy verified.

- srh-axspa-treatment: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). First-line continuous NSAID therapy, failure of >=2 NSAIDs prompting biologic therapy, TNF inhibitors (infliximab, adalimumab, etanercept, certolizumab, golimumab), IL-17A/F inhibitors (secukinumab, ixekizumab, bimekizumab) and paradoxical IBD exacerbation, JAK inhibitors (tofacitinib, upadacitinib), inefficacy of conventional synthetic DMARDs for axial disease, total hip arthroplasty indications, spinal fracture vigilance in fused spine verified.
- srh-enteropathic: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Type 1 peripheral arthritis (pauciarticular, parallels IBD activity) vs Type 2 (polyarticular, independent of IBD course), axial spondyloarthritis in Crohn and ulcerative colitis (HLA-B27 associated in ~50%), avoidance of NSAIDs in active bowel disease, anti-TNF monoclonal antibodies (infliximab, adalimumab) effective for both gut and joints, contraindication of etanercept and IL-17 inhibitors for active Crohn disease, ustekinumab (IL-12/23) and JAK inhibitors, Whipple disease Tropheryma whipplei PCR and PAS staining verified.
- srh-osteoarthritis: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Pathogenesis of OA (chondrocyte senescence, cartilage fibrillations, osteophytes, subchondral sclerosis), Heberden nodes (DIP) and Bouchard nodes (PIP), first CMC square hand, Kellgren-Lawrence radiographic grading scale I-IV, core non-pharmacological therapies (exercise, weight loss), topical NSAIDs preferred first-line, intra-articular glucocorticoids vs hyaluronic acid, total joint arthroplasty triggers verified.
- srh-osteonecrosis: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Avascular necrosis etiologies (high-dose glucocorticoids, alcohol, sickle cell, SLE, trauma), femoral head blood supply (medial femoral circumflex artery), Ficat and Arlet staging I-IV, MRI double line sign and crescent sign on plain radiography, core decompression in pre-collapse stages (Ficat I-II), total hip arthroplasty in post-collapse disease (Ficat III-IV), bisphosphonate-related osteonecrosis of the jaw (BRONJ) risk factors verified.
- srh-lupus-nephritis: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). ISN/RPS classification of lupus nephritis (Class I to VI), Class III/IV proliferative glomerulonephritis activity and chronicity indices, induction regimens (cyclophosphamide Euro-Lupus / NIH vs mycophenolate mofetil), maintenance with MMF or azathioprine, belimumab and voclosporin as add-on therapies, Class V membranous nephropathy management, target proteinuria reduction (<0.5-0.7 g/day by 12 months), repeat renal biopsy indications verified.
- srh-aps: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Sapporo / Sydney criteria and 2023 ACR/EULAR classification criteria for APS, laboratory criteria (lupus anticoagulant, anticardiolipin, anti-beta-2-glycoprotein-1 on >=2 occasions 12 weeks apart), triple positivity high thrombosis risk, secondary thromboprophylaxis with warfarin (target INR 2.0-3.0; DOACs inferior in arterial/triple positive), obstetric APS management (aspirin + prophylactic/therapeutic LMWH), catastrophic APS (CAPS) triad and treatment (anticoagulation + steroids + plasma exchange / IVIG) verified.
- srh-ssc-organ: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Systemic sclerosis organ involvement: interstitial lung disease (NSIP pattern, HRCT ground glass, FVC and DLCO decline), scleroderma renal crisis (SRC, malignant hypertension, microangiopathic hemolytic anemia, ACE inhibitor treatment lifesaving, glucocorticoid trigger >15 mg/day), pulmonary arterial hypertension (Group 1, right heart catheterization, DETECT algorithm), gastrointestinal dysmotility (esophageal aperistalsis, GAVE watermelon stomach, SIBO, pseudo-obstruction), cardiac myocardial fibrosis and conduction blocks verified.
- srh-mctd-overlap: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Mixed connective tissue disease (MCTD) criteria (Alarcon-Segovia, Kasukawa), high-titer anti-U1-RNP antibodies, absence of anti-Sm and anti-dsDNA, clinical features (Raynaud, swollen puffy hands, synovitis, myositis, sclerodactyly), pulmonary hypertension as leading cause of mortality, overlap myositis with anti-PM-Scl and anti-Ku, Rhupus syndrome (features of RA and SLE), corticosteroid responsiveness verified.
- srh-ctd-ph: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Group 1 PAH in connective tissue disease (highest prevalence in limited cutaneous SSc), 2022 ESC/ERS diagnostic hemodynamic criteria (mPAP >20 mmHg, PAWP <=15 mmHg, PVR >2 Wood units), DETECT screening algorithm (FVC/DLCO ratio, NT-proBNP, ECG, telangiectasias), upfront dual oral combination therapy (ERA + PDE-5i), high-risk disease parenteral prostacyclin requirement, distinction from Group 3 PH due to ILD (PH-ILD, inhaled treprostinil approval via INCREASE trial) verified.
- srh-takayasu: Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse). Takayasu arteritis large vessel granulomatous panarteritis, 2022 ACR/EULAR classification criteria (age <=60 at onset, imaging of LVV, clinical scoring >=5 points), Hata angiographic classification types I-V, renovascular hypertension from renal artery stenosis (30-50%), aortic regurgitation from root dilatation, MRA/CTA/PET-CT imaging for mural thickening and edema, high-dose glucocorticoids + early steroid-sparing agents (MTX, AZA, tocilizumab, anti-TNF), surgical revascularization delayed until remission verified.

Takayasu arteritis (pulseless disease: chronic granulomatous panarteritis predominantly affecting the aorta, its primary branches: subclavian, carotid, renal, and pulmonary arteries, occurring characteristically in young females under 40 years), 2022 ACR/EULAR classification criteria (mandatory entry criteria: age at onset <=60 years and imaging evidence of large vessel vasculitis; Type IIa: ascending aorta, arch, and branches; Type IIb: Type IIa plus thoracic descending aorta; Type III: thoracic descending aorta, abdominal aorta, and/or renal arteries; Type IV: abdominal aorta and/or renal arteries; Type V: combined IIb and IV, most common), renal artery stenosis (occurs in 30-50%, typically ostial/proximal, producing severe renovascular hypertension via RAAS activation, which may be masked if measured in claudicating, pulseless upper extremities), cardiovascular complications (major causes of mortality: severe aortic regurgitation from root dilatation in up to 25%, coronary ostial stenosis, congestive heart failure, pulmonary hypertension), non-invasive vascular imaging (MRA, CTA, 18F-FDG PET-CT have replaced catheter angiography; MRA detects early mural edema: concentric wall thickening and delayed mural enhancement prior to fixed luminal narrowing), medical induction therapy (high-dose prednisone 1 mg/kg/day; relapses occur in 50-70% on tapering, warranting early steroid-sparing immunosuppressants: methotrexate, azathioprine, leflunomide, mycophenolate mofetil), refractory disease therapy (anti-TNF monoclonal antibodies infliximab/adalimumab or IL-6 receptor antagonist tocilizumab; surgical or endovascular revascularization strictly delayed until systemic inflammatory disease is quiescent). 2 bank figures. | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; Granulomatosis with polyangiitis (GPA, Wegener granulomatosis: necrotizing granulomatous vasculitis of small and medium vessels), classical clinical triad (upper airway, lower respiratory tract, and glomerulonephritis), c-ANCA targeting proteinase 3 (PR3-ANCA in 85-90% of generalized disease; primed neutrophil surface expression with respiratory burst and endothelial necrosis), upper airway destruction (>90% involvement: chronic rhinosinusitis, epistaxis, septal perforation with saddle-nose collapse, subglottic stenosis), pulmonary involvement (bilateral nodular and cavitary infiltrates in up to 50%, thick-walled cavities, alveolar hemorrhage from necrotizing capillaritis), renal biopsy findings (pauci-immune necrotizing crescentic glomerulonephritis with absence of significant immune complex deposition on IF), induction therapy (RAVE trial: rituximab 375 mg/m2 weekly x 4 equivalent to cyclophosphamide, superior for relapsing disease), and steroid-sparing/maintenance (ADVOCATE trial: oral avacopan C5aR antagonist 30 mg bid non-inferior to steroid taper at 26 weeks and superior at 52 weeks; rituximab 500 mg q6m maintenance from MAINRITSAN trial). IgA vasculitis (Henoch-Schonlein purpura: immune complex small-vessel vasculitis characterized by galactose-deficient IgA1 Gd-IgA1 deposition), classical tetrad (non-thrombocytopenic palpable purpura with lower extremity/buttock predilection, colicky abdominal pain with ileo-ileal intussusception risk, arthralgias, and glomerulonephritis), renal biopsy features (mesangial proliferation with dominant granular IgA and C3 deposits, histologically identical to primary IgA nephropathy/Berger disease), hypocomplementemic urticarial vasculitis syndrome (HUVS, McDuffie syndrome: chronic urticarial lesions >24-48h with burning/pain, profound hypocomplementemia with low C1q, C3, C4, and anti-C1q autoantibodies), skin biopsy differentiation (true leukocytoclastic vasculitis with postcapillary venular fibrinoid necrosis and neutrophil karyorrhexis, distinct from non-vasculitic urticarial wheals), HUVS mortality risk (accelerated severe panacinar pulmonary emphysema and COPD in young patients), pediatric IgA vasculitis supportive therapy (hydration, analgesia, steroids for severe abdominal/joint pain without nephritis prevention), and severe disease immunosuppression (high-dose steroids plus MMF, cyclophosphamide, or rituximab for crescentic nephritis or systemic HUVS). | srh-behcet | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Behcet disease (Silk Road variable-vessel vasculitis characterized by recurrent orogenital aphthosis, ocular inflammation, and unique arterial and venous thrombosis), ICBD 2014 criteria (oral aphthae 2 pts, genital aphthae 2 pts, ocular lesions 2 pts; skin lesions 1 pt, vascular lesions 1 pt, CNS lesions 1 pt, positive pathergy 1 pt; score >=4 points confirms diagnosis with >94% sensitivity), genetic association (HLA-B51, specifically HLA-B*5101 in 50-80% along ancient Silk Road conferring 5- to 6-fold risk), pathergy test (intradermal 20-22G forearm puncture producing sterile papule/pustule >=2 mm with erythematous halo at 24-48 hours), ocular manifestations (50-70% incidence: non-granulomatous panuveitis, retinal vasculitis, and gravity-shifting sterile hypopyon causing irreversible visual loss), angio-Behcet (venous thrombosis: superficial phlebitis, DVT, Budd-Chiari, SVC syndrome; pulmonary artery aneurysms PAA as arterial hallmark with fatal rupture risk), neuro-Behcet (parenchymal brainstem-diencephalic encephalitis with poor prognosis vs non-parenchymal cerebral venous sinus thrombosis with isolated intracranial hypertension), and therapy for severe manifestations (high-dose glucocorticoids with cyclophosphamide or anti-TNF monoclonal antibodies infliximab/adalimumab; absolute contraindication to anticoagulation alone in untreated pulmonary artery aneurysms). Two bank figures (beh-et-syndrome-fig1, neuro-behcet-fig1). | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against 2014 ICBD criteria, EULAR Behcet recommendations, and Harrison 22e Ch 363. | srh-vasculitis-mimics | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Thromboangiitis obliterans and vasculitis mimics: Buerger disease (non-atherosclerotic segmental inflammatory vasculopathy of distal limbs in smokers <45 years), pathology (acute hypercellular inflammatory thrombi with microabscesses and multinucleated giant cells preserving internal elastic lamina and vessel wall architecture), clinical features (digital ischemia, Raynaud, rest pain, superficial migratory thrombophlebitis, absent distal pulses; complete tobacco/nicotine cessation as sole arrested progression), cholesterol atheroembolism (post-catheterization/anticoagulation/thrombolysis atheroembolic shower with livedo reticularis, blue toes with preserved pulses, acute renal failure, eosinophilia), histology (biconvex needle-shaped cholesterol clefts with foreign-body giant cell reaction), left atrial myxoma (constitutional IL-6 secretion causing fever, arthralgias, ESR rise mimicking vasculitis, plus tumor embolization causing gangrene/stroke), calciphylaxis (calcific uremic arteriolopathy in ESRD/dialysis: medial calcification and intimal proliferation of subcutaneous arterioles with painful ischemic purpura progressing to black eschars on fat areas), and levamisole-induced vasculopathy (retiform purpura/necrosis on ears/face/extremities, agranulocytosis, dual MPO/PR3 p-ANCA and c-ANCA positivity). Systemic manifestations of sarcoidosis: multisystem granulomatous disorder (>90% pulmonary/intrathoracic lymphadenopathy), Lofgren syndrome acute triad (erythema nodosum, bilateral hilar lymphadenopathy, migratory polyarthritis/periarthritis with 80-90% spontaneous remission), Heerfordt syndrome uveoparotid fever (anterior uveitis, parotid enlargement, low-grade fever, facial cranial nerve VII palsy), Scadding staging (Stage 0 normal, I BHL alone, II BHL + parenchymal infiltrates, III parenchymal infiltrates alone, IV end-stage pulmonary fibrosis), non-caseating granuloma histology (tightly packed epithelioid histiocytes, CD4+ T-cell rim, Schaumann and asteroid bodies without caseous necrosis), dysregulated calcium metabolism (macrophage 1-alpha-hydroxylase producing excess 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D calcitriol, hypercalciuria exceeding hypercalcemia), cardiac sarcoidosis (conduction blocks, VT, sudden death; late gadolinium enhancement MRI and 18F-FDG PET), and first-line therapy (prednisone 20-40 mg/day tapered over 6-12 months; methotrexate 10-20 mg/week as primary steroid-sparing agent). | srh-ocular-rheum | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Ocular manifestations of rheumatic disease: acute anterior uveitis AAU (most common extra-articular feature of axial SpA/ankylosing spondylitis in 30-40%: acute unilateral painful red eye, photophobia, ciliary flush, sterile hypopyon, HLA-B27 association), juvenile idiopathic arthritis-associated uveitis (oligoarticular ANA-positive young females: chronic asymptomatic insidious bilateral anterior uveitis requiring strict slit-lamp screening to prevent band keratopathy, cataracts, glaucoma, and vision loss), scleritis (painful deep boring eye pain radiating to temple/forehead, violaceous scleral injection failing to blanch with topical phenylephrine 2.5%, strongly associated with GPA, RA, and relapsing polychondritis), episcleritis (benign superficial sectoral fiery injection that blanches readily with phenylephrine, mild irritation without deep pain), peripheral ulcerative keratitis PUK (crescentic marginal corneal thinning/melting from immune-complex occlusive vasculitis in active RA or GPA requiring systemic immunosuppression), keratoconjunctivitis sicca (secondary Sjogren in RA/SLE/SSc: tear film breakup time <10s, Schirmer test <=5 mm in 5 min, ocular surface rose bengal / lissamine green staining), and neuro-ophthalmic ischaemia (arteritic anterior ischaemic optic neuropathy A-AION in giant cell arteritis: chalky white swollen optic disc with sudden painless catastrophic visual loss). | srh-hypermobility | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Hypermobility and heritable connective tissue disorders: Beighton score (9-point hypermobility scale: passive 5th MCP dorsiflexion >90 deg 2 pts, passive thumb apposition to forearm 2 pts, elbow hyperextension >10 deg 2 pts, knee hyperextension >10 deg 2 pts, forward trunk flexion palms flat on floor 1 pt; generalized hypermobility defined as >=5 in young adults, >=6 in children, >=4 in adults >50 years), hypermobile Ehlers-Danlos syndrome hEDS (2017 international criteria: clinical diagnosis requiring generalized joint hypermobility, systemic manifestations of connective tissue fragility, and exclusion of other heritable conditions), vascular Ehlers-Danlos syndrome vEDS (autosomal dominant COL3A1 mutation in type III procollagen: arterial dissections/ruptures, hollow organ bowel/uterine rupture, translucent skin, acrogeria, life expectancy ~50 years), classical Ehlers-Danlos syndrome cEDS (COL5A1/COL5A2 mutations: marked skin hyperextensibility >1.5 cm at volar forearm, widened atrophic 'cigarette-paper' or 'papyraceous' scars, joint hypermobility), Marfan syndrome (autosomal dominant FBN1 mutation encoding fibrillin-1: revised 2010 Ghent criteria requiring aortic root aneurysm Z-score >=2.0, ectopia lentis upward/temporal, systemic score >=7 points, and family history), Loeys-Dietz syndrome (TGFBR1, TGFBR2, SMAD3, TGFB2 mutations: arterial tortuosity, widespread aneurysms with high rupture risk at smaller diameters, hypertelorism, bifid uvula / cleft palate), osteogenesis imperfecta (COL1A1/COL1A2 mutations in type I collagen: bone fragility, recurrent fractures, blue sclerae, dentinogenesis imperfecta, conductive hearing loss; Sillence classification types I-IV), and cardiovascular monitoring (baseline and regular surveillance echocardiography and aortic imaging; celiprolol in vEDS; beta-blockers or ARBs in Marfan). | srh-malignancy-rheum | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Rheumatic manifestations of malignancy and checkpoint inhibitors: cancer-associated myositis (15-25% of adult dermatomyositis/polymyositis cases represent a paraneoplastic syndrome peaking within 3 years of onset; autoantibodies anti-TIF1-gamma and anti-NXP2 carry high positive predictive value for underlying malignancy requiring age/gender-appropriate screening), hypertrophic osteoarthropathy HOA (Pierre Marie-Bamberger syndrome: digital clubbing, symmetric painful non-inflammatory periostitis of tubular long bones, and joint effusions most often secondary to non-small cell lung cancer NSCLC), pathophysiology of HOA (pulmonary arteriovenous shunting or tumor production causing circulating platelet precursors / megakaryocytes to impact in peripheral nailbeds, releasing PDGF and VEGF driving periosteal proliferation and angiogenesis), imaging in HOA (plain radiographs showing symmetric circumferential 'onion-skin' periosteal new bone formation along distal diaphysis/metaphysis of tibia, fibula, radius, ulna; 99mTc bone scan revealing classic symmetric cortical 'double-stripe' sign), immune checkpoint inhibitor-induced arthritis irAE (inflammatory arthritis occurring in 3-10% treated with anti-PD-1, anti-PD-L1, or anti-CTLA-4; presents as polymyalgia rheumatica-like or RA-like symmetric polyarthritis responsive to low-to-moderate glucocorticoids), severe and life-threatening immune checkpoint inhibitor irAEs (fatal triad of myositis, myocarditis, and myasthenia gravis-like overlap with elevated troponin, CK, and heart block requiring immediate permanent ICI cessation, pulse methylprednisolone 1 g/day x 3 days, and plasma exchange, abatacept, or IVIG), paraneoplastic remitting seronegative symmetrical synovitis with pitting edema RS3PE (elderly acute symmetric polysynovitis with prominent bilateral hand/foot dorsal pitting edema, elevated VEGF, and dramatic glucocorticoid responsiveness), and palmar fasciitis and polyarthritis syndrome (woody induration of palms with flexion contractures, predominantly associated with ovarian carcinoma). Three bank figures (hypertrophic-osteoarthropathy-fig1, hypertrophic-pulmonary-osteoarthropathy-fig1, dermatomyositis-fig1). | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against ASCO/NCCN ICI toxicity guidelines, IMACS criteria, and Harrison 22e Ch 72/365. | srh-novel-therapies | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Novel immunotherapies and cell therapy: CD19 CAR T-cell therapy (achieves profound deep immune system reset in refractory severe SLE, idiopathic inflammatory myositis, and systemic sclerosis by comprehensively eliminating tissue-resident and circulating autoreactive B cells and plasma cell precursors, producing drug-free sustained remission), anifrolumab (fully human IgG1kappa monoclonal antibody directed against subunit 1 of type I interferon receptor IFNAR1; blocks downstream phosphorylation of STAT1/STAT2 induced by all type I interferons IFN-alpha, beta, omega; FDA/EMA approved for moderate-to-severe SLE with cutaneous and musculoskeletal efficacy), avacopan (oral highly selective competitive small-molecule antagonist of human complement C5a receptor C5aR1/CD88; in phase 3 ADVOCATE trial successfully replaced high-dose systemic glucocorticoids in ANCA-associated vasculitis, improving eGFR and minimizing steroid toxicities), obinutuzumab (type II glycoengineered anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody inducing enhanced direct non-apoptotic cell death and superior ADCC compared to type I rituximab; phase 2 NOBILITY trial proved significantly enhanced complete renal response in proliferative lupus nephritis), telitacicept (recombinant dual-target fusion protein of TACI receptor extracellular domain fused to human IgG1 Fc; simultaneously neutralizes both BLyS/BAFF and APRIL, suppressing abnormal B-cell and plasma cell maturation in SLE and RA), deucravacitinib (oral first-in-class selective allosteric inhibitor of TYK2 binding unique regulatory pseudokinase JH2 domain; blocks IL-23, IL-12, and type I interferon signaling without inhibiting JAK1, JAK2, or JAK3), dapirolizumab pegol (investigational pegylated Fab fragment targeting CD40 ligand CD40L/CD154 lacking Fc domain, eliminating past platelet activation and thromboembolism risks while blocking T-B costimulation in SLE), and BTK inhibitors (fenebrutinib and remibrutinib selectively inhibiting downstream BCR and Fc-receptor signaling in B cells and myeloid cells, undergoing phase 3 evaluation in SLE, Sjogren, and chronic spontaneous urticaria). Two bank figures (interferonopathy-fig1, lupus-nephritis-fig1). | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against ADVOCATE, NOBILITY, BLISS trials, and Harrison 22e Ch 350. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| srh-drug-monitoring | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Drug toxicity monitoring: Methotrexate monitoring (baseline CBC, liver enzymes ALT/AST, creatinine; repeat every 2-4 weeks for initial 3 months, then every 8-12 weeks; hold MTX if transaminases rise >2-3x ULN, dose reduction/folic acid optimization; discontinue for pulmonary toxicity / acute pneumonitis), Leflunomide monitoring (monthly blood pressure monitoring due to ~10% secondary hypertension risk, liver function tests and CBC every 8-12 weeks; cholestyramine 8 g tid for 11 days rapid washout for planned pregnancy or severe toxicity), Hydroxychloroquine retinopathy screening (American Academy of Ophthalmology AAO 2016 guidelines: baseline fundus examination, annual screening after 5 years with automated 10-2 visual fields and spectral-domain OCT; high-risk daily dose >5.0 mg/kg actual body weight, renal impairment, concomitant tamoxifen), Janus kinase inhibitor safety monitoring (FDA boxed warning: major adverse cardiovascular events MACE, venous thromboembolism DVT/PE, serious infections, malignancies, all-cause mortality from ORAL Surveillance trial; baseline/periodic CBC, fasting lipid profile, and TB/HBV/HCV screening), IL-6 receptor antagonists tocilizumab/sarilumab (fasting lipid profile at 4-8 weeks, routine CBC for neutropenia and thrombocytopenia; caution regarding masked CRP/fever in serious infection and lower GI intestinal perforation risk in diverticulitis), TNF inhibitor contraindications and monitoring (absolute contraindication in NYHA Class III-IV heart failure or LVEF <50%, active untreated mycobacterial or severe fungal infection, demyelinating disease history MS / optic neuritis; baseline IGRA, HBV serologies, annual TB risk evaluation), Calcineurin inhibitors cyclosporine and tacrolimus (routine 12-hour trough blood concentration monitoring, serial serum creatinine, potassium, and blood pressure monitoring for nephrotoxicity, hyperkalemia, and hypertension), and Corticosteroid baseline and long-term monitoring (baseline DEXA scan for T-score, FRAX assessment, calcium/vitamin D supplementation, bisphosphonate prophylaxis for high fracture risk, blood glucose, lipid profile, ophthalmology screening for posterior subcapsular cataract and open-angle glaucoma). Two bank figures (srh-drug-monitoring-fig1, immunosuppressants-calcineurin-biologics-jak-fig1). | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against AAO 2016 HCQ guidelines, ACR/EULAR DMARD monitoring recommendations, and Harrison 22e Ch 350. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| srh-trials | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1953. Landmark trials in rheumatology: BeSt trial (randomized multicenter trial in early active RA comparing sequential monotherapy, step-up combination, initial combination with infliximab, and initial combination with tapered high-dose prednisone; demonstrated initial combination therapy achieves faster clinical improvement and less radiographic damage at 2 years), ORAL Surveillance trial (post-marketing safety trial comparing tofacitinib 5 mg or 10 mg bid versus TNF inhibitors in RA patients >=50 years with >=1 cardiovascular risk factor; demonstrated non-inferiority was NOT met for MACE and malignancies, leading to class-wide regulatory warnings for JAK inhibitors), RAVE trial (randomized non-inferiority trial in severe ANCA-associated vasculitis comparing rituximab 375 mg/m2 weekly x 4 vs oral cyclophosphamide 2 mg/kg/day; established rituximab non-inferior for overall remission induction and superior for relapsing disease), PEXIVAS trial (largest randomized trial in severe ANCA-associated vasculitis n=704; demonstrated plasma exchange did NOT reduce death or end-stage kidney disease, while reduced-dose glucocorticoid regimen was non-inferior to standard-dose with fewer serious infections), ADVOCATE trial (randomized trial in ANCA vasculitis comparing avacopan 30 mg bid vs prednisone taper; established avacopan non-inferior for remission at week 26 and superior for sustained remission at week 52 with significant improvement in eGFR), BLISS-LN trial (randomized phase 3 trial in active lupus nephritis adding intravenous belimumab 10 mg/kg to standard therapy; demonstrated significantly higher Primary Efficacy Renal Response at week 104 43% vs 32% with reduced renal-related events or death), SENSCIS trial (randomized double-blind trial in systemic sclerosis-associated ILD; demonstrated oral nintedanib 150 mg bid reduced annual rate of decline in forced vital capacity FVC by 44% vs placebo), and CARES vs FAST trials (conflicting CV safety trials comparing febuxostat vs allopurinol in gout; CARES reported higher all-cause and cardiovascular mortality with febuxostat, while FAST demonstrated non-inferiority in 6,128 patients for CV events, mortality, and heart failure). Two bank figures (rapidly-progressive-gn-fig1, gout-and-other-crystal-associated-fig2). | ss-rheumatology-immunology blueprint (PR #1948), Kelley & Firestein 11e / Harrison 22e | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against published landmark trials (NEJM/Lancet) and Harrison 22e. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-skin-grafts | all 9 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Skin Grafts: STSG, FTSG and Graft Take Biology: Split-thickness skin grafts STSG (contain epidermis and variable superficial dermis: thin 0.008-0.012 inches, medium 0.012-0.018 inches, thick 0.018-0.024 inches; harvested with dermatome), Full-thickness skin grafts FTSG (contain epidermis and entire dermis with adnexal structures: minimal secondary contracture, superior cosmetic match, but requires highly vascularized bed), phases of skin graft take (plasmatic imbibition 24-48 hours, inosculation 48-72 hours, and revascularization/capillary ingrowth beyond day 4-5), plasmatic imbibition (passive capillary absorption of fibrinogen and tissue fluid leading to 20-40% graft weight gain before vascular connection), inosculation (micro-anastomoses and direct alignment between pre-existing graft capillaries and recipient bed capillary buds around day 3), primary contraction (immediate elastic recoil upon harvest driven by dermal elastin fibers, maximal in FTSG), secondary contraction (wound bed myofibroblast-mediated contracture during healing, maximal in thin STSG), graft failure etiology (hematoma is the single most common cause of skin graft failure, lifting graft from bed; infection and shearing also major causes), and composite grafts (skin plus subcutaneous cartilage or fat, e.g. auricular helical rim or alar reconstruction, strictly limited to <=1.5 cm diameter due to reliance on peripheral bed revascularization). Two bank figures (plastic-surgery-skin-grafts-flaps-and-reconstruction-fig1, reconstructive-ladder-grafts-flaps-fig1). | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 17 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 1 Ch 17. STSG thickness ranges (0.008-0.024 in), three phases of graft take (imbibition 24-48h, inosculation 48-72h, revascularization), primary vs secondary contraction biology, hematoma as #1 cause of graft failure, and composite graft 1.5 cm limit verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-tissue-expansion | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Tissue Expansion: Physiology, Calculations and Complications: biological and mechanical creep (tissue expansion recruits new tissue via mechanical stretch, biological creep involves cellular proliferation, increased mitotic activity, and collagen synthesis, distinct from mechanical creep which is instant fluid displacement/dermal realignment), histological changes in expanded tissue (epidermis thickens with hyperkeratosis and increased mitotic activity, dermis markedly thins with fragmented elastin, adnexal density decreases, muscle thins and atrophies, thick vascularized pseudocapsule forms around expander), capsule biology (highly vascularized fibrous capsule forms with inner synovial-like layer and outer collagen layer rich in myofibroblasts, capable of capsule contracture or capsulotomy for expansion gain), expander selection and dimensions (base width of expander should equal or slightly exceed base width of defect; rectangular expanders yield 38-40% surface area gain, crescentic 32-35%, round 25-30%), filling protocol and inflation calculation (saline inflation starts 2-3 weeks postoperatively after secure wound healing; weekly serial inflations of 10-15% expander volume guided by capillary refill >2-3s and skin tension), total expansion volume formula (ideal expander base dimensions calculated so flap advancement distance equals defect dimension plus generous surplus for contracture; flap length = 2x defect width), major complications (infection, extrusion/exposure, hematoma, seroma, device failure/valve rupture, flap necrosis; exposure before adequate volume requires explantation or salvage depending on infection status), and external vs internal injection ports (internal remote subcutaneous dome avoids external tract infection but risks accidental expander puncture; external port avoids needle puncture pain but carries higher retrograde contamination risk). Visual tables provided. | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 18 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 1 Ch 18. Biological creep cellular proliferation, epidermal thickening with dermal thinning, expander shape gain yields (rectangular ~38% > round ~25%), weekly 10-15% inflation rate, and extrusion salvage protocols verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-burns-hand | all 9 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Acute Burn Resuscitation, Parkland Formula and Inhalation Injury: total body surface area TBSA estimation (Wallace Rule of Nines: head/neck 9%, each upper limb 9%, anterior torso 18%, posterior torso 18%, each lower limb 18%, perineum 1%; Lund and Browder chart mandatory in pediatric burns adjusting for large head-to-body proportion), Parkland and Baxter resuscitation formulas (Parkland: 4 mL/kg/%TBSA lactated Ringer's in first 24 hours; modified Brooke: 2 mL/kg/%TBSA; half administered in first 8 hours from TIME OF INJURY, remaining half over next 16 hours), resuscitation titration endpoints (hourly urine output target 0.5 mL/kg/h in adults, 1.0-1.5 mL/kg/h in children <30 kg; avoiding fluid creep leading to abdominal compartment syndrome bladder pressure >20 mmHg or orbital compartment syndrome), inhalation injury diagnosis (facial burns, singed nasal vibrissae, soot in sputum/oropharynx, hoarseness, stridor; fiberoptic bronchoscopy confirms airway mucosal edema, ulceration, and soot deposition), carbon monoxide and cyanide poisoning (affinity of CO for Hb 200-250x oxygen causing left shift of oxyhemoglobin curve and falsely normal pulse oximetry SpO2; treated with 100% normobaric oxygen cutting half-life from 320 to 60-90 min, or hyperbaric oxygen HBO for carboxyhemoglobin >25% or neuro symptoms; cyanide treated with hydroxocobalamin 5 g IV binding cyanide to cyanocobalamin), burn wound depth classification (superficial 1st deg: epidermis only, erythema, painful, no blisters, heals 3-5d; superficial partial 2nd deg: papillary dermis, blisters, wet pink, exquisitely painful, heals 10-14d; deep partial 2nd deg: reticular dermis, white/mottled, diminished sensation, heals >3w with scarring; full thickness 3rd deg: entire dermis/subcutis, leathery, painless, waxy white or charred, requires excision and grafting), circumferential full-thickness extremity and chest burns (leathery non-yielding eschar causing tourniquet effect and compartment syndrome or thoracic restriction; emergency bedside escharotomy without anesthesia incising mid-lateral/mid-medial lines through eschar into subcutaneous fat), topical antimicrobial agents (silver sulfadiazine SSD 1% causes transient leukopenia, contraindicated near eyes/face and sulfa allergy; mafenide acetate 8.5-11% penetrates deep cartilage/eschar but causes metabolic acidosis via carbonic anhydrase inhibition and severe burning pain; silver nitrate 0.5% causes electrolyte leaching hyponatremia/hypochloremia and black staining), and electrical and chemical burn peculiarities (high-voltage electrical >1000 V causes deep muscle necrosis, rhabdomyolysis, myoglobinuria, renal failure requiring urine target 1.0-2.0 mL/kg/h with sodium bicarbonate alkalinization; hydrofluoric acid causes systemic hypocalcemia, treated with topical/injected calcium gluconate). Two bank figures (burn-wound-depth-classification-and-histology-fig1, burn-types-management-pearls-fig1). | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 25 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 4 Ch 25, ABA guidelines, and ATLS 10e. Parkland formula timing from burn onset, 0.5 mL/kg/h urine output goal, bronchoscopy gold standard for inhalation, CO Hb-affinity 200-250x and hydroxocobalamin cyanide antidote, full-thickness painless escharotomy indications, and mafenide carbonic anhydrase acidosis verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-lower-extremity-reconstruction | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Gustilo Fractures, Lower Extremity Defect Coverage and Free Flaps: Gustilo-Anderson classification of open tibial fractures (Type I clean wound <1 cm; Type II wound 1-10 cm without extensive soft-tissue stripping; Type III >10 cm with extensive soft-tissue injury or high energy; Type IIIA adequate periosteal coverage; Type IIIB extensive soft-tissue loss with periosteal stripping requiring flap coverage; Type IIIC open fracture with arterial injury requiring vascular repair), lower extremity reconstructive thirds (Godina principles: upper third defect covered with gastrocnemius muscle flap; middle third defect covered with soleus muscle flap; lower third and foot defect requiring microvascular free tissue transfer due to scarce local pliable tissue), gastrocnemius muscle flap (medial head more commonly used due to greater length and arc of rotation; based on medial sural artery branch of popliteal artery; covers proximal third tibia and knee joint), soleus muscle flap (dominant proximal pedicles from posterior tibial and peroneal arteries with segmental distal perforators; hemisoleus or whole soleus covers middle third tibia), free flap choices for lower extremity reconstruction (anterolateral thigh ALT flap based on descending branch of lateral circumflex femoral artery: versatile fasciocutaneous/myocutaneous flap with long pedicle and low donor morbidity; latissimus dorsi muscle flap for massive soft-tissue defects; gracilis muscle flap for medium cavity defects), recipient vessel selection in traumatized lower limb (end-to-side microvascular anastomosis preferred to maintain distal axial foot perfusion; anastomosis performed outside zone of trauma; posterior tibial artery most commonly utilized), timing of soft tissue coverage (Godina classic timeline: emergency coverage <72 hours, delayed 72h to 3 months, late >3 months; modern consensus emphasizes definitive reconstruction within 7 days to dramatically lower deep infection, chronic osteomyelitis, and non-union rates), and mangled extremity severity score MESS (>=7 points predicting amputation with high specificity based on skeletal/soft-tissue injury, limb ischemia, shock, and patient age; clinical judgment balances salvage vs early amputation). Visual tables provided. | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 38 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 4 Ch 38 and Orthopaedic Trauma Association standards. Gustilo IIIA/B/C definitions, thirds rule (gastrocnemius upper, soleus middle, free flap lower), sural artery pedicle, ALT descending branch LCFA, end-to-side vessel anastomosis outside trauma zone, 7-day reconstruction window, and MESS >=7 cutoff verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-craniosynostosis | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Craniosynostosis: Vault Deformities and Syndromic Management: isolated non-syndromic craniosynostosis patterns (sagittal synostosis most common ~50%: scaphocephaly/dolichocephaly with increased AP diameter; coronal unicoronal synostosis: anterior plagiocephaly with harlequin eye deformity; bicoronal synostosis: brachycephaly with wide flat forehead; metopic synostosis: trigonocephaly with triangular forehead and hypotelorism; lambdoid synostosis: posterior plagiocephaly with trapezoid head shape and mastoid bulge, differentiated from positional plagiocephaly which has parallelogram head and anterior ear displacement), Virchow law of skull growth (premature fusion of a cranial suture restricts skull expansion perpendicular to the fused suture, while compensatory overgrowth occurs parallel to the fused suture), syndromic craniosynostosis genetics (Crouzon syndrome: FGFR2 mutation, bicoronal synostosis, severe proptosis, midface hypoplasia, normal hands; Apert syndrome: FGFR2 mutation Ser252Trp/Pro253Arg, bicoronal synostosis, midface hypoplasia, severe complex symmetric syndactyly of hands and feet 'mitten hands/rosebud'; Pfeiffer syndrome: FGFR1/FGFR2 mutations, broad thumbs and great toes; Saethre-Chotzen syndrome: TWIST1 mutation, low frontal hairline, ptosis, ear crus abnormalities; Muenke syndrome: FGFR3 Pro250Arg mutation), raised intracranial pressure ICP in craniosynostosis (single suture carries 15-20% risk of elevated ICP, multiple/syndromic sutures carry >40-60% risk; presents with papilledema, headaches, cognitive developmental delay, and copper-beaten skull appearance on radiographs), surgical timing and techniques (endoscopic strip craniectomy with postoperative helmet molding therapy performed early at 2-4 months of age; open fronto-orbital advancement and cranial vault remodeling FOAR performed at 9-12 months of age), fronto-orbital advancement and remodeling FOAR (advancement of supraorbital bar bandeau by 10-15 mm to expand anterior fossa, correct proptosis, and restore forehead contour), midface hypoplasia advancement in syndromic cases (Le Fort III osteotomy or monobloc frontofacial advancement with rigid external distraction RED frame or internal distractors performed at 6-10 years or earlier for obstructive sleep apnea or globe subluxation), and chiari I malformation and hydrocephalus (progressive skull-base disproportion causing tonsillar herniation in Crouzon/Pfeiffer requiring posterior vault distraction or foramen magnum decompression). Two bank figures (craniosynostosis-fig1, scaphocephaly-sagittal-craniosynostosis-fig1). | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 30 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 3 Ch 30 and craniofacial consensus guidelines. Virchow law perpendicular restriction, sagittal scaphocephaly 50% frequency, Apert vs Crouzon FGFR2 distinctions (syndactyly mitten hands in Apert), Pfeiffer broad thumbs/toes, ICP risks, 2-4m endoscopic vs 9-12m open FOAR timing, and Le Fort III distraction verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |
| spl-nasoethmoid-orbital-fractures | all 8 steps | Lane C stage 2 lesson, PR #1958. Nasoorbitoethmoid Complex and Orbital Blowout Fractures: NOE complex anatomy and central fragment (confluence of nasal bone, frontal process of maxilla, internal angular process of frontal bone, and ethmoid bone; medial canthal tendon MCT attaches to anterior and posterior lacrimal crests; central fragment is the bone segment bearing the MCT insertion), Markowitz and Manson NOE classification (Type I: single large non-comminuted central fragment bearing intact MCT; Type II: comminuted central fragment but MCT remains attached to bone fragment; Type III: severe comminution of central fragment with MCT avulsion or comminution extending into anterior cranial fossa), clinical hallmarks of NOE fractures (traumatic telecanthus with intercanthal distance >35 mm [normal 30-34 mm; telecanthus diagnosed when intercanthal exceeds half interpupillary distance], saddle nose deformity due to loss of dorsal nasal support, and CSF rhinorrhea from cribriform plate fracture / dural tear), orbital blowout fracture mechanism and anatomy (blunt trauma to globe raising intraorbital hydraulic pressure causing fracture of thinnest orbital walls; posteromedial orbital floor medial to infraorbital nerve and lamina papyracea of ethmoid bone most commonly fractured, while sturdy infraorbital rim remains intact), clinical presentation and muscle entrapment (vertical diplopia, restriction of upward gaze, positive forced duction test differentiating mechanical entrapment of inferior rectus / inferior oblique / periorbital fibroadipose tissues from neurogenic palsy, enophthalmos, periorbital ecchymosis, and infraorbital nerve V2 hypoesthesia), white-eyed blowout fracture in pediatric patients (orbital trapdoor fracture in children with elastic bone that recoils and severely entraps rectus muscle with minimal external signs; presents as medical/surgical emergency triggering oculocardiac reflex: bradycardia, nausea, syncope; requires urgent surgical release within hours to prevent ischemic muscle necrosis), surgical indications for orbital floor repair (persistent diplopia with positive forced duction test at 1-2 weeks, symptomatic enophthalmos >2 mm, large orbital floor defect >50% of floor area causing tissue herniation into maxillary sinus, or trapdoor pediatric entrapment), and surgical approach and reconstruction materials (transconjunctival incision with retrocaruncular extension or subciliary incision; autologous bone/cartilage or alloplastic implants including porous polyethylene Medpor sheets, titanium mesh, or PLLA resorbable plates; transnasal canthopexy wiring passing superior and posterior to lacrimal crest for NOE telecanthus). One bank figure (facial-fractures-fig1). | ss-plastic-surgery blueprint (PR #1955), Plastic Surgery 5E Ch 28 | Checked (gemini-3.8-flash-low; writer muse) | Clean fact-check against Neligan Plastic Surgery 5e Vol 3 Ch 28 and AO CMF craniomaxillofacial standards. Markowitz-Manson Types I-III MCT attachment, telecanthus >35 mm, posteromedial floor/lamina papyracea vulnerability, positive forced duction, pediatric trapdoor oculocardiac reflex emergency, 50% floor / 2 mm enophthalmos surgical bars, and transnasal wiring verified accurate. Passes checkLesson [] and 100% mustKnow coverage. |

Ischemic vs non-ischemic pathophysiology, blood gas thresholds (pH <7.25 vs >7.40), phenylephrine dilution and dosing protocol, distal needle (Winter) vs open (Al-Ghorab) shunts, proximal shunts, and 36-48 hr smooth muscle necrosis window with early prosthesis verified accurate. Prevalence 3% term and up to 30% preterm infants; spontaneous descent rare beyond 3-6 months; orchidopexy performed between 6 and 12 months (completed before 18 months) to preserve spermatogenesis; diagnostic laparoscopy gold standard for non-palpable testis; Fowler-Stephens orchidopexy (single or two-stage) dividing main testicular vessels with deferential artery collateral preservation; 4- to 8-fold increased relative risk of testicular malignancy (seminoma most common); patent processus vaginalis in >90% requiring high ligation; bilateral non-palpable testes with normal male phallus evaluated as urgent CAH/DSD (karyotype, 17-OHP, electrolytes); hormonal therapy (hCG/GnRH) not routinely recommended (<15% efficacy). | fmt-virginity-pregnancy | step 2 | OUTDATED LAW: teaches the MTP Act 1971 limits (one doctor up to 12 weeks, two doctors 12 to 20, contraception failure for married women only). The MTP (Amendment) Act 2021 allows one doctor up to 20 weeks, two doctors 20 to 24 weeks for specified categories, beyond 24 weeks only via a medical board for substantial fetal abnormality, and contraception failure for any woman or her partner. || sns-chiari-syrinx-cvj | all 8 steps | Lane B lesson, PR #1997 branch ss-lessons-B-neurosurgery (tip cdb5ca57e). Same-family deep-read vs ss-neurosurgery blueprint mustKnow: Chiari I 5 mm descent with small posterior fossa, 50 percent syrinx, cough headache, downbeat nystagmus, hand wasting; symptomatic-plus-syrinx FMD with duraplasty vs observed incidental ectopia; Chiari II with myelomeningocele, beaked tectum, shunting first; syrinx cape dissociated loss with spared touch, Charcot joints, spinothalamic pattern; syrinx resolves after flow restoration, shunt only loculated post-traumatic/post-infective; basilar invagination past Chamberlain and McGregor lines, posterior reduction-fixation; Goel C1-C2 instability with joint distraction, no transoral when reducible; Traynelis AOD highly lethal, occipitocervical fusion. checkLesson [] ; tx 40-90 words and say under 120 all 8 steps; narration varied natural speech. R2 200 x2, both viewed srcs exist. QUERY for different-family checker (no same-family edit): step-7 AOD trauma step carries spina-bifida-fig1 captioned for Chiari II open defects; drawing is accurate but does not illustrate AOD or occipitocervical fusion. | AANS/CNS Chiari + syringomyelia; ISPN Chiari II; Goel CVJ doctrine; Traynelis 1986; blueprint PR #1996 | UNCHECKED (muse writer; same-family runner muse-spark-1.3, no valid blind pass) | No text errors found vs blueprint.
